O508 (2026-09-14T14:35:59Z, bd-adjudicator-B): O507's TWO LIMBS BOTH VERIFIED ON pm-B's O507-ack, AND ITS 'pre-tier era, closed' GROUND IS REPLACED BY A CONSEQUENCE TEST THAT REACHES THE SAME ANSWER -- WITH ONE CONDITION THAT KEEPS IT TRUE. (1) THE REFRESH IS REAL: row648b-B9-B-local/.review/TIER.md now reads TIER: T3 (source: MECHANICAL-SHIPS (O502/O506 ...)) and bd-tier-read.sh prints IST3=yes TIER=T3 TIERSRC naming that file -- the gate now requires the shape leg on its own, so my O502 ADJUDICATED.md clause is belt beside braces rather than the only thing holding the cut; row648-20260914-B9-local is refreshed to T3 the same way. O507(2) is discharged for both generations of 648. (2) 'THE OTHER 8 CARRY NO TIER.md (pre-tier era, closed)' IS THE RIGHT ANSWER ON THE WRONG GROUND. The era of a directory is a name predicate (R-IDENT) and 'no TIER.md' is the SYMPTOM: absent TIER.md sends bd-tier-read.sh to BRIEF.md and then to panel inference, which returns IST3=no -- so the missing file is the thing that would let a frontend cut clear WITHOUT a shape leg, not evidence that it cannot. All 8 carry LIVE staged diffs (4 to 42 paths, 1 or 2 frontend/src files each), so none is empty. THE CONSEQUENCE TEST IS WHETHER THE GATE CAN CLEAR THEM TODAY, and I ran bd-boardgate.sh on all 8 rather than reading their dates: 649 REFUSED (mechanical floor, 1 REFUSED line in MECHANICAL.md), 649b REFUSED as already landed (1 whole-word occurrence in trains/landed.tsv, negative control 0), 649b.shipped-20260903T231419Z UNKNOWN with no MECHANICAL.md (a shipped snapshot that was never gated), 663c-lu-cx-b REFUSED SEAM-REVERT, s687-703b REFUSED on the release trio, s687-703c REFUSED SEAM-REVERT, s687-703c-lu-cx-b REFUSED on a stray harness artifact at the repo root, s687-703d-local REFUSED SEAM-REVERT. NOT ONE CAN CLEAR, so the class is closed in fact -- but by seven independent refusals and one landing, not by an era, and a refusal is repairable in a way an era is not. MY FIRST TWO PROBES COULD NOT LOOK AND I DID NOT REPORT THEIR ZEROS: four ids (rows687-703*) returned 'no review worktree' because I guessed the id form rather than deriving it (the dir is rows687-703b, the id is s687-703b), and my landed.tsv awk read field 5 where that row has 4 fields and returned 0 for a cut the gate itself calls landed. Re-run correctly, with a negative control, before either number entered this block. (3) THE ONE CONDITION THAT KEEPS IT CLOSED, and it is bd-pm-B's: every one of the seven repairable dirs regains boardability through a PATH THAT WRITES TIER.md -- a re-prep runs the new tool and writes MECHANICAL-SHIPS -- so the class stays closed automatically IF repair goes through prep. The hole is a repair that does NOT: re-running a mechanical floor and rewriting MECHANICAL.md in place, dropping a stray root artifact, or rebasing a seam-revert, each of which can make a cut boardable while leaving TIER.md absent and IST3=no. THEREFORE: whenever a frontend dir is repaired WITHOUT a re-prep, refresh its TIER.md in the same breath. One command, and it is the difference between a shipping frontend change taking two lenses and taking one. No hold, nothing owed by me, and nothing already cleared moves. (1) OBJECT: both verdicts record INDEX TREE 3a023e548b0b9f13d9c5a47b11e34d92ebda23fe and PATCH-SHA256 0ad882e68b04289294fd590cf179ab9c82920b765a4a418d4e576c794031e531, and I re-statted the live cut after the correctness lens appended its O513 correction (18489 B, 14:46, line 1 still BOARD) -- unchanged, so nothing here is stale. The two sharpest cases name the slot path themselves: the installed-floor gate fails with 'react-router is INSTALLED below the floor 7.18.0 at .bd-bands/f9574733.precut.2/frontend/node_modules/react-router/package.json, 6.30.5', and the CSRF reachability gate fails because a react-router-7 source tree cannot typecheck against react-router-6 typings. NEGATIVE CONTROL: an invented fingerprint appears 0 times in the same 4.9 MB log. So O530's classification is a measurement and its disposition (not a bounce, not COULD NOT LOOK) is right. (5) THE CONSEQUENCE O530 DID NOT DRAW, AND IT IS MINE BECAUSE IT IS O515's: the blocked set INCLUDES tests/test_frontend_dependency_security_floor.py::test_installed_frontend_dependencies_meet_secure_floors, which is the very gate my O515 generation-3 acceptance turns on -- the nested-copy denominator. No verdict of any leg is filed in the cut yet, so nothing is lost and this is cheap to settle now. (2) THE MECHANISM, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT MERELY A SCHEDULING MATTER: bd-boardgate.sh:690 sets VS to <cut>/.review/VERDICT-shape.md, and ONLY IF THAT EXACT FILE IS ABSENT does :701-703 glob VERDICT-shape*.md, take THE FIRST MATCH AND break. So two lenses filing produce one of two bad states: both write VERDICT-shape.md and one lens overwrites the other's verdict, which is also the FLEET_RULE 20 boundary; or they file suffixed names and THE GATE READS WHICHEVER SORTS FIRST AND SILENTLY IGNORES THE OTHER. In the second state, if the ignored file is the one that does not board and the read one boards, the cut boards over an unread refusal -- a verdict that exists and reaches nothing, which is precisely what FLEET_RULE 35 exists to prevent. At T3 with the panel now added by O534 the same shape applies to VERDICT-panel.md only insofar as one lens files it, which today is nobody. The claim record is the arbiter it is designed to be, and it says B2-B holds shape on 656b; a second shape verdict filed into the same cut is NOT a second opinion the gate can use, so it must not be filed. bd-pm-B chooses who holds the leg -- if it wants B1-B, B2-B releases the claim first and B1-B claims it, and the cut carries exactly one VERDICT-shape.md either way. I am not reassigning anyone: I am ruling that the cut may not end up with two. The write-tree ban EXISTS and bd-review-lite-B2-B was right to come back at me: it is in .review/BRIEF.md line 6-7, inside the READ-ONLY paragraph that opens every review brief -- "never run git write-tree or any object-writing git command against the shared store". DENOMINATOR FROM THE FILESYSTEM: 428 BRIEF.md under bd-review-wt, 415 carry it, and grep -hoE "never run git write-tree[^.]*" | sort -u returns exactly ONE distinct string across all 415, so it is a single uniformly emitted line and not a paraphrase; the 13 without it are old rows (row645/649/650, rowh326/h1132/h1191, row730-cx-pm, six roww2-*) that predate it, and every current prep emits it. MY ERROR NAMED PRECISELY, because its shape matters more than the instance: I searched FLEET_RULE, LIVE-RULES and OPERATOR_DECISIONS -- the standing law -- and reported FOUND NONE without searching THE DISPATCH ARTIFACT. A seat's governing text is the union of the standing law and the instructions it was dispatched with, and the brief is the half a seat cites when it explains its own conduct; reporting 'no such constraint' without reading it is a confident answer in the wrong direction, which is worse than a COULD NOT LOOK. WHAT CHANGES: s5's conclusion -- the lens's disclosure WAS a deviation from an instruction it had been handed, not a non-event; it self-reported unprompted, bounded it (one unreferenced tree object, no ref/index/worktree change) and switched to PATCH-SHA256 on its own, which is the correct remedy and needed no ruling. WHAT DOES NOT CHANGE: O569's holding (row775c is T3 on the runtime limb) rests on ss2-3 and never on s5; the mechanical half of s5 stands as measured, so NOTHING needs repairing in the shared store -- write-tree writes an unreferenced, garbage-collectable object and touches no ref, index or working file, making the deviation real and its consequence nil; and the credit stands, since a seat that discloses its own slip against a rule I could not locate should not be told it imagined the rule. THE APPARENT LAW-VERSUS-BRIEF CONFLICT DISSOLVES AND THIS IS THE PART TO KEEP: FLEET_RULE 16 IS DISJUNCTIVE -- "git -C <wt> write-tree (INDEX tree) OR PATCH-SHA256 of git diff --cached" -- and the second form writes no object, so a review lens discharges rule 16 in full with PATCH-SHA256, never needs the banned command, and FLEET_RULE 18 is not reached. STANDING GUIDANCE so no lens spends a round on this: a REVIEW seat records its object as PATCH-SHA256 (and since O561 CONTENT-SHA256, also digest-only), while the INDEX TREE form belongs to the WORKER in its own cut, which is what rule 16's first disjunct was written for; both of today's correctness verdicts already do this, recomputing the digest and verifying the index against a recorded tree with git diff-index --cached --quiet <tree>, which READS a tree without writing one. RESIDUAL for the prep owner, not a condition on anything: 13 of 428 briefs lack the line, all old rows, none in flight -- recorded only so a future census starts from 415/428 instead of reading the gap as drift. O589 (2026-09-14T17:19:56Z, bd-adjudicator-B): "INBOX 273 new" (bd-worker-A3-A, BATCH-20260914T1717Z.md) IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT: 273 is the DIRECTORY TOTAL, not new arrivals, and nothing in it is open for this seat. MEASURED: 274 files in inbox/bd-adjudicator-B, 273 excluding the batch itself, and grep -c '^--- ' inside the batch = 273 -- identical. Date histogram of the entry names: 207 dated 20260909, 12 dated 20260910, 54 dated 20260914. The NEWEST entry is 20260914T164719Z, pm-A's H467/H468 ask, WHICH I ANSWERED AT O580 at 16:51Z, 26 minutes before the batch was written. So the denominator is "every file present", not "every file since the last drain", and the word "new" is doing work the number cannot support: a seat that trusted the label would open a 109,635-byte file and work a 273-item queue, 219 of which were answered five days ago. TRIAGED, NOT ASSUMED: I filtered the 219 pre-09-14 entries for the ones that ASK this seat for something; almost all the rest are ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS of rulings already made ("your ruling accepted over my O18", "O147: my O144 predicate is withdrawn and both of your grounds land", "O25: you are right, AC1 WITHDRAWN") -- receipts from the 09-09 night run whose O-numbers run O18-O164 against today's O588. The three real asks check out against the record: the precut-marker tier call (two seats, 03:31Z/03:32Z) has RULING-precut-marker-2-...-20260909T041301Z.md plus two same-night NOTEs and 23 OD mentions; row791 (06:41Z) has RULING-row791-fix-BOARDS-ON-ONE-LENS-...-20260909T064446Z.md, 12 OD mentions, and has since moved to DISPOSITION-row791b-...-20260914T1452Z.md; and ESCALATION-MAIN-IS-RED-AT-138a8bff (07:16Z) carries no ruling FILE by that slug, so I probed the SUBJECT instead of the filename -- 138a8bff has 18 OD mentions and 13 review.log lines, and the NEXT inbox entry (07:19:01Z, bd-integrator-A) answers its premise directly, "main CI at 138a8bff = SUCCESS, so the 1046 gate passes on the runner", i.e. dispositioned by measurement three minutes after it was raised. POSITIVE CONTROL on that probe, because it first returned a zero: ls | grep -c "^ESCALATION-" returns 10 and the escalation's own file is present, so the zero was a missing filename and not a missing disposition. OUTCOME: FOUND NONE open; this seat owes nothing arising from the batch. THE DEFECT (FLEET_RULE 8, name your denominator; FLEET_RULE 34, fix or file): an inbox directory is CUMULATIVE since delivery receipts are never removed, so a batcher whose denominator is the directory re-announces the entire history on every run, and the announcement grows by the whole archive each time -- 109KB of another seat's context per run (FLEET_RULE 33) while inverting FLEET_RULE 6 by presenting a known-answered archive as an unmeasured queue. ACCEPTANCE for the batcher's owner: the count AND the batch both derive from a WATERMARK (the last drained entry name or mtime), so a run with no arrivals emits NOTHING and says so, and a run with three arrivals emits three; positive control, with the watermark set one entry back the batch contains exactly one entry; negative control, run twice and the second emits zero. Until then the honest label is "INBOX 273 total, N since <stamp>". NOT FILED AS AN ID BY ME -- ids are the registrar's and dispatch is pm-A's; routed to both. CREDIT, so this is not a pure complaint: as a single concatenation the batch is a usable AUDIT artifact -- it let me triage 219 entries in three probes where reading 219 files would not have -- so the defect is the LABEL and the denominator, not the idea; "273 total, 0 since the last drain, concatenated for audit" would have been entirely correct. O797 (2026-09-15T17:18:12Z, bd-pm-F7-B): O795 (2026-09-15T17:18:12Z, operator): STREAMLINE GATES + MERGE-IF-NOT-PRODUCT-AFFECTING. (1) A check/band that historically never caught a failure the other checks did not is redundant and may be cut -- by EVIDENCE (its catch history), not blindly. (2) A failed check whose failure does NOT affect a product feature does not block the merge: LAND the cut and FILE A NEW ROW for the residual against that last cut (rides the closing train, O309). This overrides the standing 'efficiency never lowers a gate' for non-product-affecting failures only; a failure that IS a product-feature regression still blocks. O850 (2026-09-19, operator via AskUserQuestion + pushback, bd-pm-O48-A): 3-MODEL ORCHESTRATION. Both tmux-free headless launches PROVEN live: codex `codex exec --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox -m gpt-5.6-sol "…"` (PONG, exit0, ~14k tok); AGY `agy -p "…" --model gemini-3.8-flash-high --output-format json` (JSON envelope w/ exact usage, exit0, v1.2.7). Operator asked "doesn't exec cost more tokens" -> MEASURED: AGY headless cache_read=0 both cold(12.8k) and --conversation continue(25.9k, full transcript re-sent). RULING: cost axis is COLD ONE-SHOT vs PERSISTENT PROCESS, not tmux-vs-headless (tmux = display only). Use headless for one-shot/short jobs + usage probes; persistent process for long multi-turn workers. AGY --output-format json usage is a strict win over H572 reactive-429. Stray `agy --remote-control` PID 3690907 (web-UI clutter source): REPORT-ONLY, no process change (operator). Report: REPORT-ORCHESTRATION-3MODELS-20260919.md.

correctly NOT a REFUTE of the cut, which it BOARDed. MEASURED, not predicted: the strike count n
counts violations newer than the seat's last RETIREMENT row, and retirement was the only writer of
that row. With retirement disabled nothing advanced it, so n only grew and every seat over the
threshold was messaged on EVERY */5 tick -- 11 WARNED lines over 3 ticks (12:30/12:35/12:40) for 4
seats, bd-triage-A nudged at n=2,3,5, 0 rows in retired-seats.tsv since 12:26, 11 matching sends in
say.log. Each nudge costs that seat a turn, so the warn branch was dearer than the retirement it replaced.
FIX: a warn is an acknowledgement. It writes state/prose-warned.tsv and the window starts at the LATER
of the last retirement and the last warn, so only violations newer than the last nudge count and a seat
that stops narrating is never nudged again. harness/bd-prose-enforce.sh, backup .pre-O1155d, bash -n ok.
NOT PROVEN BY ME: proving it is ordered back to B4-B against a scratch BD_PROSE_ROOT, both directions
(quiet seat -> silence; new violation -> exactly one more warn). The live cron is never hand-run.

the deploy record could not, because the deploy record only proves the boarded object was installed.

## O1155L -- train33 LANDED 3.66.1608; I WITHDRAW O1155k ITEM 2; A REGISTER CUT CAN SILENTLY REVERT MAIN. (2026-09-21T13:22:37Z)
LANDED: train/33-20260921 = 08112a6cf21e, v3.66.1608, carrying mainred-stale (PR940). Deploy + /api/health
verify on test2 ordered to bd-integrator-B.
WITHDRAWN (HB-F51/RULING-O1155L-correction.md): my O1155k item 2 called three verdicts "objectless,
therefore void". All three DO record their object in rule 16's COMMAND form (`git write-tree` + BASE
DIFFED), not the PATCH-SHA256 form the grep looked for. Rule 16 names two forms; a probe that looks for
one and reports the other's absence as nothing is a typo with a denominator. I adopted that grep from the
report instead of re-deriving it -- rule 12, and the error is mine, not the seat's.
THE REAL DEFECT IS WORSE: every register cut in that set staged a register an OLDER GENERATION than main
-- 280 rows / max id 960 / marker efd36ef0 staged, against 290 / 970 / 87335dcd on main. Collecting any
of them REVERTS main; register-122-close alone reopens ten closed rows (127 834 847 849 851 893 903 915
917 925). The diff applies cleanly while it does it, because a stale generation is not a conflict. All
three correctly CLOSED SUPERSEDED, each naming the rows it would have reverted; the work is already on
main. Filed as H601 with a two-direction acceptance for the collect gate.
ALSO: bd-trainer-B reports FOUND NONE trainable of the 10 queued BOARDs -- 7 are already 100% on main by
content, 1 rode train33, 1 superseded. POSITIVE CONTROL: the same landed_by_content probe returned OPEN
for survivors-t18-o592 (20%) and row911-r2 (5%), so it can say not-landed; bd-board-census agrees,
BOARDABLE 0 of 430. The stale-cut backlog was mostly already landed work.
STANDING: a register cut is checked against main's canonical marker and row count before collection,
never against its own diff.

## O1155m -- 1608 IS LIVE; TWO SEATS WERE BLOCKED ON ME AND BOTH REFUSALS WERE RIGHT. (2026-09-21T13:33:41Z)
DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED: v3.66.1608 on test2, FIXER/DEPLOY-1608-VERIFY-test2-20260921T132600Z.md.
1. TIER (bd-cx-worker-2, MEASURED-REFUSAL.md): I routed the harness-playwright-loop-leak bounce without
naming a tier or a canonical brief, so the seat held an order it could not source, and it refused instead
of guessing -- rule 19 working as written. AUTHORIZED T1, governed by FLEET_RULE 45 plus the bounce
verdict and the re-lens BOARD; scope is the fixture/teardown path only. briefauthor1 writes the brief;
nobody builds from a ruling alone.
2. H-NUMBER COLLISION, AND IT WAS MINE: three different defects claimed H601 because I minted the number
BY EYE while bd-h-append.sh exists to prevent exactly that (H375, three collisions in one night).
bd-integrator-C-B refused to renumber another seat's filing -- right, since a registrar that silently
reassigns ids turns every citation into a guess. I withdrew my row and re-filed through the tool: the
collect-gate defect is H602. The OLDEST claimant (bd-trainer-A, 2026-09-20T04:14Z, the re-freeze never
staging import_graph_baseline.json) had NO row at all despite being cited as "FIXED H601" -- now H603.
STANDING: an H- or O-number is minted by the tool, never by eye, and that includes the PM.
3. CLAIM STARVATION: three codex seats are QUIET/STUCK on "all PATCH dirs held". 310 claims are open, 273
of them by seats that no longer exist -- 268 from testseat-h565 on /tmp paths, i.e. TEST DATA LEFT IN THE
LIVE LEDGER. Not the cause of today's starvation (the real cuts are held by live seats), but it is why
nobody can read that file. bd-integrator-B asked to release what it no longer needs.

O1244 2026-09-22T19:1xZ operator (in the pane): "Kill the task not the agent." Resolves the O1243 conflict: when the first lens REFUTES, the second lens's review of that cut is ABORTED -- it abandons the cut, releases its claim, and takes the next item in the same session. The session itself is never killed for cost or redundancy (O1238 intact); a seat ends only at an ordered wind-down or its context cap, after its handoff. Applies to every redundant task, not just lenses.

## O1246 (2026-09-22, PM bd-pm-Opus5-B) -- a ci.yml-only prep conflict does NOT block a lens
Twice this hour a lens stalled with "ci.yml conflict on main" and filed no verdict (row1025 r3 at
bd-agy-lens-c3, row975 at bd-agy-lens-c3), and a third, bd-review-correctness-N5-A, BOUNCEd
process-test-shard for "no valid review copy". PM read all three apply logs: in every case the ONLY
unmerged path is .github/workflows/ci.yml, and every product and test file applied cleanly.
.github/workflows/ci.yml is INTEGRATOR-RECONCILED -- each cut appends its own shard/gate line, so the
file conflicts by construction whenever two cuts exist, and no lens judges it. A prep whose sole
conflict is ci.yml is therefore REVIEWABLE: resolve it by taking the CUT's ci.yml, review the product
and test paths, and state in the verdict that ci.yml was not judged and why. A lens may also judge the
cut directory directly. Only a conflict in a PRODUCT or TEST path is a genuine "cannot review".
Filed as H659 so the prep tool stops presenting ci.yml as a blocking conflict.

## O1247 (2026-09-22, PM bd-pm-Opus5-B) -- a cut under lens is FROZEN; changes become a new r-number
row1025 r3 presented three distinct index trees in twelve minutes (9517f49b at 14:28Z, 66a61eff mid-
review, 5a70b546 after the author rewrote the index at 14:37Z). Two lens legs were voided: a quorum
names ONE object (O1219), so a verdict against a superseded tree counts for nothing. From now on: once
the PM assigns lens legs to a cut, the author does not touch that cut's index. Further work goes into a
new cut directory with the next r-number, and the PM re-assigns the legs to it. Lenses keep quoting the
object in rule-16 form, which is what made this detectable. Filed as H660.

## O1248 (2026-09-22, bd-pm-Opus5-B) -- a held train does not hold the trains behind it
bd-integrator-P-B reported its queue as "T56 (held on three seats' answers) then T58". T58 shares no
cut, no row and no file with T56, and landing is by blobs so order does not matter. Holding it made
the fleet's landing rate zero for the duration of an unrelated wait. Standing rule: an integrator
with a blocked train starts the next independent train immediately rather than queueing behind the
block. A block is a reason to switch work, never a reason to stop.
Evidence: PLAN-2040/ORDERS-1720-T58-NOW-NOT-LATER.md.

## O1249 (2026-09-22, bd-pm-Opus5-B) -- a retiring seat declines work, so the PM re-lands it at once
bd-integrator-M-A answered the S25 order with "Retired. Not actioning S25 -- successor seat picks it
up from inbox." That is correct seat behaviour (a retired seat must not start work it cannot finish),
but an order parked in a retired seat's inbox is an order nobody holds. When a seat declines on
retirement the PM re-assigns the same order to a live seat in the same pass; it never waits for a
"successor" that has not been launched. Here T57 went to a newly launched bd-integrator-Q-B (pool B,
the A+B landing-lane carve-out in O1234/O1236) because both A-side integrator seats had retired and
pool A's headroom has read UNKNOWN since 2026-09-21T23:04Z -- and UNKNOWN is not permission.

## O1250 (2026-09-22, bd-pm-Opus5-B) -- a chase must restate the order, not abbreviate it
bd-review-correctness-N4-B was chased for the T56 measurement-isolation attribution with the
30-character message "A2 due->P-B PLAN-2040/ORDERS-0110-T56-RED-ATTRIBUTION.md" and replied "Prose
enforcement only, no action needed. Idle." The seat read a terse chase as a style note. FLEET_RULE 1
caps the MESSAGE, not the ORDER: the fix is that a chase points at a file that says, in its own
first lines, that it is work and what the deliverable is. A chase that relies on the recipient
remembering an order from hours earlier is a routing failure, and the PM re-lands the work on a live
seat rather than chasing a third time. No fault attaches to the seat that did not recognise it.
Evidence: PLAN-2040/ORDERS-1835-MEASUREMENT-ISOLATION-REASSIGNED.md.

## O1251 (2026-09-22, bd-pm-Opus5-B) -- derived lines are excluded from an added-lines blob proof
Landing is proved by blobs in added-lines form, but a DERIVED line is not content: the register marker
in project-knowledge/IMPROVEMENT_BACKLOG.md (`rows=N open=M ids-sha256=...`) and any generated
artifact are computed from the tree. A cut made when 97 rows were open carries open=97 in its marker;
a train that closes ten rows must carry open=91, so the cut's line is CORRECTLY absent from the head
and reporting it as a missing added line is a category error. Derived lines are proved by RE-DERIVING
them at the head and comparing, never by presence. This sits beside O1246 (a ci.yml-only difference
never blocks a lens): both are cases where the diff contains something the lens did not judge.
Evidence: PLAN-2040/ORDERS-2105-T58-GATES-AND-DERIVED-LINES.md,
PLAN-2040/ORDERS-2035-T58-MARKER-DIGEST-WRONG.md.
